But no, I'm not avoiding places or activities.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling irritable lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say so. Just really frustrating and tiring.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying activities that you normally find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I haven't really noticed a change there. I mean, I still like doing my usual stuff. I might be a little distracted by everything going on with my father-in-law, but... yeah, when I actually get a minute to myself, I still enjoy the things I normally do.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty or worthless about anything lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, the whole situation is just a lot to deal with, but I don't feel like it's my fault or anything. I'm doing the best I can with it. So, no, I haven't been feeling guilty or... worthless, or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel apprehensive or fearful about the future?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I wouldn't say I'm scared of the future. It's more just... dealing with what's happening right now is hard enough. I don't really sit around worrying about what's going to happen down the road, I just... try to get through the day-to-day with the whole situation. So, no, I don't feel fearful about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble sleeping at night?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any trouble with that. I mean, by the time the day is over, I'm usually pretty worn out from dealing with everything. So I just kind of go to bed and sleep fine. I haven't noticed any issues falling asleep or staying asleep, really.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel tense most of the time?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel tense. I mean, I'm not walking around with my muscles all tightened up or feeling on edge like that. It's really just... a lot on my mind with the situation right now, but physically, no, I don't feel wound up or tense most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious even when there's no apparent reason to be?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. If I'm feeling stressed or overwhelmed, it's... well, it's because of the situation I'm dealing with. There's always a pretty clear reason for it. I don't just get anxious out of nowhere or for no reason at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious most of the time, even when your father-in-law is not around?\n\nPatient: No, not really. So yeah, I'm definitely irritable.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight lately?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed any change there. My weight has pretty much stayed the same. I mean, I haven't stepped on a scale recently, but my clothes still fit the same, so... no, I haven't gained or lost anything that I can tell.\n\nDoctor: Has something specific and upsetting happened to you recently, aside from your father-in-law moving in?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, nothing else comes to mind. It's really just... just him moving in with us. That's been the only major change lately. Everything else is pretty much the same as it always was, it's just that one thing that's kind of thrown everything off.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced something traumatic in your life?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. Nothing like that. I mean, just the normal ups and downs of life, I guess, but... no, no real trauma or anything. I've never been in a bad accident or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having thoughts that repeat over and over in your head and are difficult to stop?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I think about the situation with my father-in-law a lot, obviously, because it's stressing me out, but... no, I don't have thoughts that I can't stop or anything like that. Just regular worrying, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're on the verge of having a panic attack?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I feel stressed out and overwhelmed, but... no, I haven't had a panic attack or felt like I was about to have one. It's not that intense. Just really frustrating and tiring.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying activities that you normally find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I haven't really noticed a change there. I mean, I still like doing my usual stuff. I might be a little distracted by everything going on with my father-in-law, but... yeah, when I actually get a minute to myself, I still enjoy the things I normally do.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty or worthless about anything lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, the whole situation is just a lot to deal with, but I don't feel like it's my fault or anything. I'm doing the best I can with it. So, no, I haven't been feeling guilty or... worthless, or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel apprehensive or fearful about the future?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I wouldn't say I'm scared of the future. It's more just... dealing with what's happening right now is hard enough. I don't really sit around worrying about what's going to happen down the road, I just... try to get through the day-to-day with the whole situation. So, no, I don't feel fearful about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble sleeping at night?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any trouble with that. I mean, by the time the day is over, I'm usually pretty worn out from dealing with everything. So I just kind of go to bed and sleep fine. I haven't noticed any issues falling asleep or staying asleep, really.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel tense most of the time?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel tense. I mean, I'm not walking around with my muscles all tightened up or feeling on edge like that. It's really just... a lot on my mind with the situation right now, but physically, no, I don't feel wound up or tense most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious even when there's no apparent reason to be?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. If I'm feeling stressed or overwhelmed, it's... well, it's because of the situation I'm dealing with. There's always a pretty clear reason for it. I don't just get anxious out of nowhere or for no reason at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious most of the time, even when your father-in-law is not around?\n\nPatient: No, not really. When he's not around, I actually feel pretty normal. It's really just... when he's there, or when I have to deal with him directly, that it gets to me. But if he's out of the house or I'm just doing my own thing, I don't feel anxious, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about things even when there's nothing in particular going on that would normally cause you to worry?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. Like I said, it's really just tied to him. If there's nothing going on, and he's not around, I'm not sitting there worrying about random things. My stress is pretty much focused on the situation at hand.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
